People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess I came in because my husband made me. Him and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, but I'm not. I know for a fact they have been watching my moves on the floor and changing the pallet counts just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. They think I don't notice the numbers changing, but I do. 

Just like I noticed when they switched the shrink wrap on us. We used to use this blue-tinted wrap, which stretches way better, especially around the heavy stuff. I was moving these huge 40-pound bags of dog food yesterday... Purina, I think. My dog, Buster, he won't even touch Purina. He only likes the wet food in the little cans. My husband bought the wrong ones last week, the chicken flavor, and Buster threw it up all over the hallway rug. It's a really nice rug, too, I got it at a yard sale for like ten bucks, but I had to scrub it with baking soda for an hour just to get the smell out. Anyway, what were you asking?

No, I know I'm not making it up. That's actually why I'm here, I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy or something. But I know for a fact they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. They think I don't notice, but I do. My grandma used to make us eat the regular ones when we'd stay over at her house in the summer. She'd put sliced bananas in it to make it better, but the bananas would get all mushy in the milk. I hate mushy fruit. It's a texture thing, I think. But no, I'm not crazy, they're definitely messing with my numbers.

She used to bring them to every family reunion, but then she moved to Florida. Tampa, I think. Or maybe Clearwater. One of those places with the really bad humidity. My hair gets so frizzy in the humidity, I can't stand it. But yeah, ten months ago is when the arguments got bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the coworkers might not actually be watching you or changing the pallet counts—that maybe this could all be in your head?\n\nPatient: No, I know I'm not making it up. That's actually why I'm here, I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy or something. But I know for a fact they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. They think I don't notice, but I do. Just like I notice when the milk is left out. My husband did that yesterday, just left the gallon of 2% sitting right on the counter for hours. It gets warm so fast, and then it smells weird when you put it in cereal. I eat Cheerios every morning, the Honey Nut kind. The regular ones taste like cardboard, don't you think? My grandma used to make us eat the regular ones when we'd stay over at her house in the summer. She'd put sliced bananas in it to make it better, but the bananas would get all mushy in the milk. I hate mushy fruit. It's a texture thing, I think. But no, I'm not crazy, they're definitely messing with my numbers.\n\nDoctor: Besides the coworkers watching you and changing the pallet counts at work, do you believe other people or things are also plotting against you or trying to harm you in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, not like strangers or the government or anything weird like that. Just... well, exactly what I was just saying a second ago. It's my husband and the warehouse guys. They're the ones acting like I'm the crazy one, teaming up to watch me and mess with my pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. I know they're doing it. \n\nAnd that supervisor job pays an extra three dollars an hour, you know. Three dollars! That really adds up on a biweekly check. I was telling my sister I wanted to use the extra money to finally get a new mattress, because ours has this huge dip right in the middle on my side. It's just an old spring mattress. My sister told me to get one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a cardboard box. Have you seen those commercials? They just cut the plastic and it puffs up into a whole bed right in your room. It's wild. But she said hers smelled like weird chemicals for like two weeks after she opened it. I definitely couldn't deal with that. I have a really sensitive nose. Like, I can tell when the forklift drivers at work have been smoking on their breaks before they even get back inside the building. The smell just clings to their safety vests. The neon yellow ones, you know? They get so dirty anyway, I don't know why they don't make them in a darker color. Neon yellow just shows every single smudge of grease.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you hear this man's voice telling you to 'check it again,' do you know who this person is, or do you have any idea why he's specifically talking to you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"Sometimes when I'm in the aisle or folding clothes at home, I hear a man's voice clear as day saying, \"Nicole, check it again,\" even when nobody is near me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know who he is. It's just... it's just this man's voice, clear as day. I'll be in the aisle at work, or even just at home folding clothes, and he'll say, "Nicole, check it again." And there's nobody anywhere near me. It's so weird when I'm folding clothes, especially the towels. I have to fold them in thirds, you know? If you just fold them in half, they don't stack right in the linen closet. My husband always just rolls them up in a ball and shoves them on the shelf, and it drives me crazy. The whole stack falls over when you try to pull one out. We have these really nice dark blue ones we got for our wedding, but the color is starting to fade from washing them so much. I think our water is too hard. It leaves those white crusty spots on the showerhead, too. Have you ever tried soaking a showerhead in a bag of vinegar? My neighbor told me to do that, you just tie a plastic bag full of white vinegar around it with a rubber band and leave it overnight. It really works, but then the whole bathroom smells like a pickle jar for two days. I can't stand the smell of pickles. Especially the sweet ones. Dill is okay on a burger, I guess, but sweet pickles are just wrong. Who wants a sweet cucumber? But yeah, I don't know who the guy is, he just tells me to check it again.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, no special powers or anything crazy like that. Like I said, I came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm the crazy one, but I know for a fact they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. They just go into the system and change the numbers when I'm not looking, or they physically move the pallets. It's just regular stuff, but they're doing it to mess with me. 

They use those handheld scanners, the ones with the little laser barcode readers. You have to make sure you hear the beep when you scan it, otherwise it didn't register. Sometimes the barcode stickers get all wrinkled or torn, and then you have to type the whole SKU number in by hand. It takes forever. And the buttons on those scanners are so tiny, if you have gloves on, you always hit two buttons at once. I bought these special gloves on Amazon that have the touchscreen tips on the fingers, but they didn't even work. They were supposed to be thermal, too, because the warehouse gets freezing in the winter. Especially over by the loading docks when the bay doors are open. The wind just whips right through there. I started wearing two pairs of socks, but then my work boots feel too tight and my toes get numb. My husband said I should just buy bigger boots, but they're expensive. Good steel-toe boots are like a hundred and fifty bucks. You can't just buy the cheap ones at Walmart, they fall apart in a month and the steel toe part always digs into the top of your foot when you walk. I had a pair once that gave me a blister the size of a quarter on my heel. I had to put those squishy gel band-aids on it for weeks. But yeah, they're just using the regular scanners to change my counts. Nothing high-tech.

Good steel-toe boots are like a hundred and fifty bucks. Nothing high-tech.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen your coworkers watch you and change the pallet counts—do you think what they're doing is something that people could actually do in the normal way, or does it seem impossible or strange in a way that doesn't make sense even though you know it's happening?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, like I just told you, it's totally normal stuff they're doing. I feel like I'm repeating myself, but I only came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy. But I know for a fact they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It's not impossible or magic, it's just them being sneaky behind my back. 

And honestly, I really want that supervisor spot mostly because they get to use the front office breakroom. Have you seen the office breakroom? They have one of those fancy Keurig machines in there, not just the gross drip coffee we have out on the warehouse floor. That floor coffee tastes like burnt dirt, I swear they never wash the glass pot. It always has that brown ring baked into the bottom. I tried bringing in my own K-cups once, the vanilla caramel kind, because they smell so good when they brew. But someone stole the whole box right out of my locker. You really can't trust anyone there. My husband told me I should just buy a padlock, but those combination locks are so annoying. I can never remember the three numbers. Left, right, left... or is it right, left, right? I used to have this bike lock when I was a kid, it was bright pink and it just used a little silver key. I kept the key on a shoelace around my neck. It was one of those sparkly shoelaces, you know, with the metallic threads woven into it? I loved those. I wish they still made fun shoelaces like that for adults. Everything is just boring black and brown now.

My husband just kind of rolls them up and shoves them in the linen closet, and it drives me crazy. You can't fit as many in there if you don't fold them flat, you know? We have this really tiny closet in the hallway, the door always sticks on the carpet because the hinges are loose. I told him to fix the hinges like three weeks ago, but he's always watching those car restoration shows on TV. He thinks he's going to buy an old Mustang and fix it up, but we don't even have a garage. Where's he gonna put a Mustang? In the driveway? The HOA would throw a fit. They already sent us a letter about the trash cans being out too long. But no, the voice doesn't tell me to do bad things or anything, just tells me to check things.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the symptoms started about eight months ago when the new scanning guns came in. Before that time, did you have any major stressful events happening in your life—like problems at home, with your husband, or anything else that was really upsetting?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... actually, the arguments with my husband started getting really bad before that. Probably about ten months ago? Yeah, it was ten months ago, right when I first started talking about trying for that shift-supervisor role. He didn't think I should go for it because of the hours. \n\nHe hates when I work the late shift because he says he can't sleep when I'm not in the bed. But he sleeps just fine on the couch when he falls asleep watching TV! He fell asleep during the Super Bowl last year, right in the middle of the halftime show. I don't even know how you sleep through all those flashing lights and the music. We had made all these snacks, too. I made those little cocktail wieners in the crockpot with the barbecue sauce and grape jelly. Have you ever tried that? It sounds gross, the jelly part, but it actually makes this really good sweet and sour glaze. I got the recipe from my aunt Linda. She used to bring them to every family reunion, but then she moved to Florida. Tampa, I think. Or maybe Clearwater. One of those places with the really bad humidity. My hair gets so frizzy in the humidity, I can't stand it. But yeah, ten months ago is when the arguments got bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the coworkers might not actually be watching you or changing the pallet counts—that maybe this could all be in your head?\n\nPatient: No, I know I'm not making it up. That's actually why I'm here, I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy or something. But I know for a fact they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. They think I don't notice, but I do. Just like I notice when the milk is left out. My husband did that yesterday, just left the gallon of 2% sitting right on the counter for hours. It gets warm so fast, and then it smells weird when you put it in cereal. I eat Cheerios every morning, the Honey Nut kind. The regular ones taste like cardboard, don't you think? My grandma used to make us eat the regular ones when we'd stay over at her house in the summer. She'd put sliced bananas in it to make it better, but the bananas would get all mushy in the milk. I hate mushy fruit. It's a texture thing, I think. But no, I'm not crazy, they're definitely messing with my numbers.\n\nDoctor: Besides the coworkers watching you and changing the pallet counts at work, do you believe other people or things are also plotting against you or trying to harm you in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, not like strangers or the government or anything weird like that. Just... well, exactly what I was just saying a second ago. It's my husband and the warehouse guys. They're the ones acting like I'm the crazy one, teaming up to watch me and mess with my pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. I know they're doing it. \n\nAnd that supervisor job pays an extra three dollars an hour, you know. Three dollars! That really adds up on a biweekly check. I was telling my sister I wanted to use the extra money to finally get a new mattress, because ours has this huge dip right in the middle on my side. It's just an old spring mattress. My sister told me to get one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a cardboard box. Have you seen those commercials? They just cut the plastic and it puffs up into a whole bed right in your room. It's wild. But she said hers smelled like weird chemicals for like two weeks after she opened it. I definitely couldn't deal with that. I have a really sensitive nose. Like, I can tell when the forklift drivers at work have been smoking on their breaks before they even get back inside the building. The smell just clings to their safety vests. The neon yellow ones, you know? They get so dirty anyway, I don't know why they don't make them in a darker color. Neon yellow just shows every single smudge of grease.\n\nDoctor: When you hear this man's voice telling you to 'check it again,' do you know who this person is, or do you have any idea why he's specifically talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don't know who he is. It's just... it's just this man's voice, clear as day. I'll be in the aisle at work, or even just at home folding clothes, and he'll say, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And there's nobody anywhere near me. It's so weird when I'm folding clothes, especially the towels. I have to fold them in thirds, you know? If you just fold them in half, they don't stack right in the linen closet. My husband always just rolls them up in a ball and shoves them on the shelf, and it drives me crazy. The whole stack falls over when you try to pull one out. We have these really nice dark blue ones we got for our wedding, but the color is starting to fade from washing them so much. I think our water is too hard. It leaves those white crusty spots on the showerhead, too. Have you ever tried soaking a showerhead in a bag of vinegar? My neighbor told me to do that, you just tie a plastic bag full of white vinegar around it with a rubber band and leave it overnight. It really works, but then the whole bathroom smells like a pickle jar for two days. I can't stand the smell of pickles. Especially the sweet ones. Dill is okay on a burger, I guess, but sweet pickles are just wrong. Who wants a sweet cucumber? But yeah, I don't know who the guy is, he just tells me to check it again.\n\nDoctor: When you say the coworkers are changing the pallet counts and watching you—do you think they're doing this in ways that would be physically possible for them to do, or do you think they might be using special equipment, technology, or some kind of special powers to watch you or change the numbers?\n\nPatient: No, no special powers or anything crazy like that. Like I said, I came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm the crazy one, but I know for a fact they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. They just go into the system and change the numbers when I'm not looking, or they physically move the pallets. It's just regular stuff, but they're doing it to mess with me. \n\nThey use those handheld scanners, the ones with the little laser barcode readers. You have to make sure you hear the beep when you scan it, otherwise it didn't register. Sometimes the barcode stickers get all wrinkled or torn, and then you have to type the whole SKU number in by hand. It takes forever. And the buttons on those scanners are so tiny, if you have gloves on, you always hit two buttons at once. I bought these special gloves on Amazon that have the touchscreen tips on the fingers, but they didn't even work. They were supposed to be thermal, too, because the warehouse gets freezing in the winter. Especially over by the loading docks when the bay doors are open. The wind just whips right through there. I started wearing two pairs of socks, but then my work boots feel too tight and my toes get numb. My husband said I should just buy bigger boots, but they're expensive. Good steel-toe boots are like a hundred and fifty bucks. You can't just buy the cheap ones at Walmart, they fall apart in a month and the steel toe part always digs into the top of your foot when you walk. I had a pair once that gave me a blister the size of a quarter on my heel. I had to put those squishy gel band-aids on it for weeks. But yeah, they're just using the regular scanners to change my counts. Nothing high-tech.\n\nDoctor: When your coworkers watch you and change the pallet counts—do you think what they're doing is something that people could actually do in the normal way, or does it seem impossible or strange in a way that doesn't make sense even though you know it's happening?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I just told you, it's totally normal stuff they're doing. Everything is just boring black and brown now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Anyway, what were you asking?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that your coworkers have been watching you and changing the pallet counts to prevent you from getting the supervisor position?\n\nPatient: I'd say... it's been about eight months now. It started right around when they brought in the new scanning guns for the inventory. They're these heavy black scanners, Motorola I think. \n\nMy uncle had a Motorola flip phone back in the day, the Razr? Man, he loved that phone. He dropped it in a lake when we went fishing up at Lake George. We didn't even catch anything that day, just some tiny little sunfish that we had to throw back. Have you ever been fishing? You have to use the right bait, otherwise the fish just steal the worm right off the hook and you're just sitting there staring at the water for hours. It gets so boring. But yeah, eight months.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned hearing voices—what do these voices say to you, and do they ever tell you to do things?\n\nPatient: Well, it's usually just this one guy's voice. Like, sometimes when I'm in the aisle at the warehouse, or even just at home folding clothes, I'll hear him clear as day. He just says, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And I look around, and nobody is even near me. It's so weird when I'm folding clothes, too, because I'm really particular about how the towels get folded. My husband just kind of rolls them up and shoves them in the linen closet, and it drives me crazy. You can't fit as many in there if you don't fold them flat, you know? We have this really tiny closet in the hallway, the door always sticks on the carpet because the hinges are loose. I told him to fix the hinges like three weeks ago, but he's always watching those car restoration shows on TV. He thinks he's going to buy an old Mustang and fix it up, but we don't even have a garage. Where's he gonna put a Mustang? In the driveway? The HOA would throw a fit. I made those little cocktail wieners in the crockpot with the barbecue sauce and grape jelly. Have you ever tried that? It sounds gross, the jelly part, but it actually makes this really good sweet and sour glaze. I got the recipe from my aunt Linda. She used to bring them to every family reunion, but then she moved to Florida. Tampa, I think. Or maybe Clearwater. One of those places with the really bad humidity. My hair gets so frizzy in the humidity, I can't stand it. But yeah, ten months ago is when the arguments got bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the coworkers might not actually be watching you or changing the pallet counts—that maybe this could all be in your head?\n\nPatient: No, I know I'm not making it up. But no, I'm not crazy, they're definitely messing with my numbers.\n\nDoctor: Besides the coworkers watching you and changing the pallet counts at work, do you believe other people or things are also plotting against you or trying to harm you in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, not like strangers or the government or anything weird like that. Just... well, exactly what I was just saying a second ago. It's my husband and the warehouse guys. They're the ones acting like I'm the crazy one, teaming up to watch me and mess with my pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. I know they're doing it. \n\nAnd that supervisor job pays an extra three dollars an hour, you know. Three dollars! That really adds up on a biweekly check. I was telling my sister I wanted to use the extra money to finally get a new mattress, because ours has this huge dip right in the middle on my side. It's just an old spring mattress. My sister told me to get one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a cardboard box. Have you seen those commercials? They just cut the plastic and it puffs up into a whole bed right in your room. It's wild. But she said hers smelled like weird chemicals for like two weeks after she opened it. I definitely couldn't deal with that. I have a really sensitive nose. Like, I can tell when the forklift drivers at work have been smoking on their breaks before they even get back inside the building. The smell just clings to their safety vests. The neon yellow ones, you know? They get so dirty anyway, I don't know why they don't make them in a darker color. Neon yellow just shows every single smudge of grease.\n\nDoctor: When you hear this man's voice telling you to 'check it again,' do you know who this person is, or do you have any idea why he's specifically talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don't know who he is. It's just... it's just this man's voice, clear as day. I'll be in the aisle at work, or even just at home folding clothes, and he'll say, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And there's nobody anywhere near me. It's so weird when I'm folding clothes, especially the towels. I have to fold them in thirds, you know? If you just fold them in half, they don't stack right in the linen closet. My husband always just rolls them up in a ball and shoves them on the shelf, and it drives me crazy. The whole stack falls over when you try to pull one out. We have these really nice dark blue ones we got for our wedding, but the color is starting to fade from washing them so much. I think our water is too hard. It leaves those white crusty spots on the showerhead, too. Have you ever tried soaking a showerhead in a bag of vinegar? My neighbor told me to do that, you just tie a plastic bag full of white vinegar around it with a rubber band and leave it overnight. It really works, but then the whole bathroom smells like a pickle jar for two days. I can't stand the smell of pickles. Especially the sweet ones. Dill is okay on a burger, I guess, but sweet pickles are just wrong. Who wants a sweet cucumber? But yeah, I don't know who the guy is, he just tells me to check it again.\n\nDoctor: When you say the coworkers are changing the pallet counts and watching you—do you think they're doing this in ways that would be physically possible for them to do, or do you think they might be using special equipment, technology, or some kind of special powers to watch you or change the numbers?\n\nPatient: No, no special powers or anything crazy like that. Like I said, I came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm the crazy one, but I know for a fact they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. They just go into the system and change the numbers when I'm not looking, or they physically move the pallets. It's just regular stuff, but they're doing it to mess with me. \n\nThey use those handheld scanners, the ones with the little laser barcode readers. You have to make sure you hear the beep when you scan it, otherwise it didn't register. Sometimes the barcode stickers get all wrinkled or torn, and then you have to type the whole SKU number in by hand. It takes forever. And the buttons on those scanners are so tiny, if you have gloves on, you always hit two buttons at once. I bought these special gloves on Amazon that have the touchscreen tips on the fingers, but they didn't even work. They were supposed to be thermal, too, because the warehouse gets freezing in the winter. Especially over by the loading docks when the bay doors are open. The wind just whips right through there. I started wearing two pairs of socks, but then my work boots feel too tight and my toes get numb. My husband said I should just buy bigger boots, but they're expensive. Good steel-toe boots are like a hundred and fifty bucks. You can't just buy the cheap ones at Walmart, they fall apart in a month and the steel toe part always digs into the top of your foot when you walk. I had a pair once that gave me a blister the size of a quarter on my heel. I had to put those squishy gel band-aids on it for weeks. But yeah, they're just using the regular scanners to change my counts. Nothing high-tech.\n\nDoctor: When your coworkers watch you and change the pallet counts—do you think what they're doing is something that people could actually do in the normal way, or does it seem impossible or strange in a way that doesn't make sense even though you know it's happening?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I just told you, it's totally normal stuff they're doing. Like I said, I came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm the crazy one, but I know for a fact they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. It's just regular stuff, but they're doing it to mess with me. \n\nThey use those handheld scanners, the ones with the little laser barcode readers. I feel like I'm repeating myself, but I only came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy. Everything is just boring black and brown now.\n\nDoctor: If I told you that I checked with your warehouse and they said they haven't been changing your pallet counts or watching you, what would you think about that?\n\nPatient: I'd think they were lying to you, obviously. I am completely certain they're lying. They think I don't notice the numbers changing, but I do. \n\nJust like I noticed when they switched the shrink wrap on us. We used to use this blue-tinted wrap, which stretches way better, especially around the heavy stuff. I was moving these huge 40-pound bags of dog food yesterday... Purina, I think. My dog, Buster, he won't even touch Purina. He only likes the wet food in the little cans. My husband bought the wrong ones last week, the chicken flavor, and Buster threw it up all over the hallway rug. It's a really nice rug, too, I got it at a yard sale for like ten bucks, but I had to scrub it with baking soda for an hour just to get the smell out. Anyway, what were you asking?\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed that your coworkers have been watching you and changing the pallet counts to prevent you from getting the supervisor position?\n\nPatient: I'd say... it's been about eight months now. The voices, and that feeling that people are watching me or changing my numbers and things... yeah, about eight months. It started right around when they brought in the new scanning guns for the inventory. They're these heavy black scanners, Motorola I think. \n\nMy uncle had a Motorola flip phone back in the day, the Razr? Man, he loved that phone. He dropped it in a lake when we went fishing up at Lake George. We didn't even catch anything that day, just some tiny little sunfish that we had to throw back. Have you ever been fishing? You have to use the right bait, otherwise the fish just steal the worm right off the hook and you're just sitting there staring at the water for hours. It gets so boring. But yeah, eight months.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned hearing voices—what do these voices say to you, and do they ever tell you to do things?\n\nPatient: Well, it's usually just this one guy's voice. Like, sometimes when I'm in the aisle at the warehouse, or even just at home folding clothes, I'll hear him clear as day. He just says, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And I look around, and nobody is even near me. It's so weird when I'm folding clothes, too, because I'm really particular about how the towels get folded. My husband just kind of rolls them up and shoves them in the linen closet, and it drives me crazy. You can't fit as many in there if you don't fold them flat, you know? We have this really tiny closet in the hallway, the door always sticks on the carpet because the hinges are loose. I told him to fix the hinges like three weeks ago, but he's always watching those car restoration shows on TV. He thinks he's going to buy an old Mustang and fix it up, but we don't even have a garage. Where's he gonna put a Mustang? In the driveway? The HOA would throw a fit. My hair gets so frizzy in the humidity, I can't stand it. But yeah, ten months ago is when the arguments got bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the coworkers might not actually be watching you or changing the pallet counts—that maybe this could all be in your head?\n\nPatient: No, I know I'm not making it up. That's actually why I'm here, I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy or something. But I know for a fact they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. They think I don't notice, but I do. Just like I notice when the milk is left out. My husband did that yesterday, just left the gallon of 2% sitting right on the counter for hours. It gets warm so fast, and then it smells weird when you put it in cereal. I eat Cheerios every morning, the Honey Nut kind. The regular ones taste like cardboard, don't you think? My grandma used to make us eat the regular ones when we'd stay over at her house in the summer. She'd put sliced bananas in it to make it better, but the bananas would get all mushy in the milk. I hate mushy fruit. It's a texture thing, I think. But no, I'm not crazy, they're definitely messing with my numbers.\n\nDoctor: Besides the coworkers watching you and changing the pallet counts at work, do you believe other people or things are also plotting against you or trying to harm you in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, not like strangers or the government or anything weird like that. Just... well, exactly what I was just saying a second ago. It's my husband and the warehouse guys. They're the ones acting like I'm the crazy one, teaming up to watch me and mess with my pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. I know they're doing it. \n\nAnd that supervisor job pays an extra three dollars an hour, you know. Three dollars! That really adds up on a biweekly check. I was telling my sister I wanted to use the extra money to finally get a new mattress, because ours has this huge dip right in the middle on my side. It's just an old spring mattress. My sister told me to get one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a cardboard box. Have you seen those commercials? They just cut the plastic and it puffs up into a whole bed right in your room. It's wild. But she said hers smelled like weird chemicals for like two weeks after she opened it. I definitely couldn't deal with that. I have a really sensitive nose. Like, I can tell when the forklift drivers at work have been smoking on their breaks before they even get back inside the building. The smell just clings to their safety vests. The neon yellow ones, you know? They get so dirty anyway, I don't know why they don't make them in a darker color. Neon yellow just shows every single smudge of grease.\n\nDoctor: When you hear this man's voice telling you to 'check it again,' do you know who this person is, or do you have any idea why he's specifically talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don't know who he is. It's just... it's just this man's voice, clear as day. I'll be in the aisle at work, or even just at home folding clothes, and he'll say, \"Nicole, check it again.\" And there's nobody anywhere near me. It's so weird when I'm folding clothes, especially the towels. I have to fold them in thirds, you know? If you just fold them in half, they don't stack right in the linen closet. My husband always just rolls them up in a ball and shoves them on the shelf, and it drives me crazy. The whole stack falls over when you try to pull one out. We have these really nice dark blue ones we got for our wedding, but the color is starting to fade from washing them so much. I think our water is too hard. My neighbor told me to do that, you just tie a plastic bag full of white vinegar around it with a rubber band and leave it overnight. Dill is okay on a burger, I guess, but sweet pickles are just wrong. Who wants a sweet cucumber? But yeah, I don't know who the guy is, he just tells me to check it again.\n\nDoctor: When you say the coworkers are changing the pallet counts and watching you—do you think they're doing this in ways that would be physically possible for them to do, or do you think they might be using special equipment, technology, or some kind of special powers to watch you or change the numbers?\n\nPatient: No, no special powers or anything crazy like that. Like I said, I came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm the crazy one, but I know for a fact they've been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts so I don't get that supervisor spot. They just go into the system and change the numbers when I'm not looking, or they physically move the pallets. It's just regular stuff, but they're doing it to mess with me. \n\nThey use those handheld scanners, the ones with the little laser barcode readers. You have to make sure you hear the beep when you scan it, otherwise it didn't register. Sometimes the barcode stickers get all wrinkled or torn, and then you have to type the whole SKU number in by hand. It takes forever. And the buttons on those scanners are so tiny, if you have gloves on, you always hit two buttons at once. I bought these special gloves on Amazon that have the touchscreen tips on the fingers, but they didn't even work. They were supposed to be thermal, too, because the warehouse gets freezing in the winter. Especially over by the loading docks when the bay doors are open. The wind just whips right through there. I started wearing two pairs of socks, but then my work boots feel too tight and my toes get numb. My husband said I should just buy bigger boots, but they're expensive. Good steel-toe boots are like a hundred and fifty bucks. You can't just buy the cheap ones at Walmart, they fall apart in a month and the steel toe part always digs into the top of your foot when you walk. But yeah, they're just using the regular scanners to change my counts. Nothing high-tech.\n\nDoctor: When your coworkers watch you and change the pallet counts—do you think what they're doing is something that people could actually do in the normal way, or does it seem impossible or strange in a way that doesn't make sense even though you know it's happening?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I just told you, it's totally normal stuff they're doing. I feel like I'm repeating myself, but I only came in here because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy. No, not really. It's just the warehouse thing. Honestly, I'm only even talking to you because my husband and the guys at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy.
